115年 10月賦稅收入初步統計 
一、整體賦稅收入 
（一）實徵淨額 
10 月實徵淨額 5,669 億元，較上年同月增加 1,495 億元；累計 1 至
10 月實徵 4 兆 1,383 億元，較上年同期增加 8,499 億元(+25.8%)，
主要由證交稅、營所稅及營業稅所貢獻。隨新版財政收支劃分法實
施，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率由 61.2%降為 4.5%(獲配稅額隨之減
少 2,662 億元)，致中央政府稅收增加數 3,906 億元(+15.8%)，不及
地方政府增加數 4,671 億元(+68.5%)。 
（二）預算達成率 
累 計1 至 10 月 實 徵 淨 額 占 全 年 預 算 數 91.4% ， 其 中 中 央 政 府 為
92.8%，地方政府為87.7%；與全年預算數差距分別為3,913億元、
2,227億元、1,614億元。 
圖 1 賦稅實徵淨額及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）10 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營利事業所得稅增加 1,133 億元、
證券交易稅增加 220 億元及綜合所得稅增加 120 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,297
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 2,986 億元及營業稅增加 1,272 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-10 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)10 月實徵淨額 4,144 億元，較上年同月增加 1,133 億元(+37.6%)，
主因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 1 兆 4,172 億元，較上年同期增加 2,986
億元(+26.7%)，主因結算申報自繳、暫繳稅款俱增及延分期稅款
陸續入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
10 月實徵淨額 614 億元，較上年同月增加 120 億元(+24.3%)，主因
扣繳稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 8,894 億元，較上年同
期增加 910 億元(+11.4%)，主因結算申報自繳稅額增加，以及盈餘分

配、薪資所得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
(1)10月受進口代徵稅額增加及適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅增加
之交互影響，營業稅實徵淨額為負77億元，較上年同月(負146億元)
增加69億元。
(2)累 計1 至10 月 實 徵 淨額6,153 億 元 ， 較 上年 同 期 增 加1,272 億元
(+26.1%)，主因內需穩定，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口
代徵稅額增加，惟外銷營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

4.貨物稅
10月實徵淨額73億元，較上年同月減少41億元(-35.6%)，累計1至10
月實徵淨額948億元，較上年同期減少248億元(-20.7%)，主因新購汽
機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增加，加以汽柴油減徵幅度擴大所致。
5.證券交易稅
(1)10 月及累計 1 至 10 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 1,210
億元、1 兆 1,437 億元，分別較上年同期(6,267 億元、4,632 億元)
增 78.9%、1.5 倍。
(2)10 月證交稅實徵淨額 523 億元， 較上年同月增加 220 億元
(+72.6%)；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 5,583 億元，較上年同期增加
3,297 億元(+1.4 倍)。
6.關稅
10 月實徵淨額 132 億元，較上年同月增加 7 億元(+6.0%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 1,356 億元，較上年同期增加 88 億元(+6.9%)，均因機
械及電機設備、基本金屬、塑橡膠及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
10 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月減少 11 億元(-31.9%)，主因大額

稅款案件減少；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 234 億元，較上年同期增加
32 億元(+15.9%)，主因大額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
10 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+5.9%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 560 億元，較上年同期減少
11 億元(-2.0%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.使用牌照稅
10 月實徵淨額 6 億元，較上年同月減少 1 億元(-10.2%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 676 億元，較上年同期減少 1 億元(-0.1%)，主因開徵
輛數減少，以及稅款入帳時間差異所致。
（三）前 10月主要稅目實徵淨額占全年預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%(已提前達成全年預算目標)者有遺產稅(123%)、贈與稅
(107%)、印花稅(106%)、房屋稅(105%)及營所稅(102%)。
2.低於 100%者有牌照稅(99%)、營業稅(94%)、綜所稅(93%)、契稅
(90%)、關稅(88%)、菸酒稅(81%)、證交稅(80%)、貨物稅(79%)、土

增稅(71%)及特銷稅(47%)等。

三、政府別賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 10 月中央政府賦稅收入(不含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵淨
額 2 兆 8,579 億元(占全年預算數 92.8%)，較上年同期增加 3,906 億元
(+15.8%)，主因新版財政收支劃分法施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率
大幅調降，獲配稅額隨之減少 2,662 億元，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加
3,297 億元、3,306 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 10 月地方政府賦稅
收入(含中央統籌分配稅款)實
徵淨額 1 兆 1,487 億元(占全年
預算數 87.7%)，較上年同期增
加 4,671 億元(+68.5%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配之營業稅
增加 4,013 億元所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 10 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 220 億元，較
上年同期增加 4 億元(+1.7%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 144
億元，較上年同期減少 6 億元(-4.0%)；撥入長照基金 953 億元(其中 575

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、220 億元來自菸稅，158 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.2%)。

輝達會計調查疑慮升溫，全球股市承壓、避險需求攀升 
                                   2026年 11月 21日 
一、 市場動態 
1. 美股延續弱勢，半導體類股跌幅尤為顯著：美國股市 11月 20日開盤後延續弱勢，盤
中主要股價指數震盪走低，尾盤賣壓進一步擴大，其中科技股表現最為疲弱，輝達、
AMD、Broadcom及 Micron等半導體個股同步重挫，賣壓亦擴散至伺服器、網路設
備及其他 AI 基礎建設相關企業。終場道瓊工業指數下跌 1.93%，收 48,147.33 點；
S&P 500 指數下跌 3.70%，收 6,910.39 點；那斯達克綜合指數下跌 5.28%，收
22,617.73點；費城半導體指數重挫 10.73%，收 8,408.59點。產業表現方面，科技
、工業及原物料類股跌幅較大，民生必需消費及醫療保健類股表現相對抗跌。 
2. 全球股市重挫帶動避險需求，美債殖利率全面回落：11月 20日美國公債價格走揚，
10年期公債殖利率單日下跌 10個基點至 4.95%，重新回落至 5%以下。另一方面，
VIX指數明顯攀升至 37附近，顯示市場波動程度進一步升高。整體而言，當日全球金
融市場呈現明顯的股債表現分化，科技及半導體類股跌幅尤為顯著，政府公債則獲得
避險買盤青睞，市場資金配置呈現由風險性資產轉向高品質防禦性資產的跡象。 
表一：11/20美股主要指數與美國公債 10年期利率表現 
指數 收盤點位／殖利率 單日變動 漲跌幅／基點

那斯達克綜合指數 22,617.73 -1,261.47點 -5.28%

二、 下跌原因 
1. 輝達 SEC調查方向進一步明朗，企業財務資訊可信度面臨挑戰：11月 17日 WSJ已
披露 SEC正在調查輝達，但當時尚未掌握具體調查方向。11月 20日 Reuters引述兩
名知情人士指出，SEC執法人員正在檢視輝達可能存在的會計問題，為此次調查的重
要新進展。相較於先前市場主要關注管理層異動、公司治理及出口管制，此次消息使
投資人進一步擔憂企業營收認列、財務揭露及交易安排。輝達在消息公布前股價已明
顯走弱，報導發布後跌幅進一步擴大，顯示會計調查消息為加劇輝達股價賣壓的重要
催化因素，亦可能加重市場對 AI產業財務透明度的疑慮。惟目前 SEC尚未正式公布
具體調查範圍，亦未認定輝達存在會計違規。

### Page 2

4. AI投資回報疑慮延續，Anthropic掛牌未能扭轉市場情緒：隨著大型雲端業者持續擴
大資料中心投資，設備折舊、電力及融資成本同步增加，市場開始關注 AI服務收入能

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
否支應相關支出。Anthropic在上市首日上漲 17.1%後，次日股價下跌 9.1%，亦引發
資金排擠及獲利了結疑慮，反映市場對 AI產業的評價標準已由營收成長轉向投資回報
及自由現金流，對 AI相關資產的評價逐漸轉趨審慎。
5. 科技股持倉集中與去槓桿壓力，可能放大市場跌勢：AI相關產業過去為全球股市主要
上漲動能，當市場風險偏好快速下降，基金同步減碼及風險控制策略調整部位，可能
使賣壓擴散至其他營運相對穩健的企業。11月 20日美國 10年期公債殖利率由前一
日約 5.04%降至 4.93%，VIX指數則升至約 37，顯示市場風險偏好明顯下降。半導體

類股同步重挫，反映資金正降低科技股集中曝險。當企業治理、政策及估值疑慮同時
存在時，短期股價可能出現超過基本面變化幅度的調整。
6. Fed升息預期維持高檔，科技股評價與融資成本承壓：Fed官員近期偏鷹談話，使市
場對 12月升息幅度及 2027年貨幣政策路徑的不確定性升高。高利率除壓低成長型企
業未來獲利的折現價值，亦增加資料中心營運商的利息支出。CoreWeave及 Nebius
等高度依賴外部融資的企業，對利率與信用市場變化尤其敏感。
三、 市場看法
1. 輝達調查事件提高評價不確定性，實質影響取決於調查範圍：本次新增風險在於市場
開始質疑企業財務資訊可信度，使投資人即使面對優於預期的財報，仍可能要求更高
的風險補償。若後續調查僅涉及財務揭露方式，對企業實質獲利的影響可能相對有限
；若涉及營收認列、財務數據修正或重大法律責任，則須重新評估輝達的獲利能力及
合理評價。短期應優先關注公司正式聲明、SEC調查進度及會計師相關意見。
2. AI需求尚未確認反轉，但短線修正風險仍高：輝達近期財報仍顯示先進運算設備需求

具一定支撐，尚不能單憑股價重挫認定 AI產業已進入衰退。後續除追蹤 Microsoft、
Amazon、Alphabet及 Meta等大型科技業者 2027年資本支出計畫，亦應檢視設備
交付、實際收款及終端運算需求。若資本支出維持成長且 AI服務收入持續增加，產業
中期基本面仍具支撐；反之，若客戶融資能力下降並導致訂單延後，則須留意修正由
評價面轉向基本面。
3. 半導體股同步重挫，不代表產業基本面將同步惡化：當市場避險需求急升時，投資人
可能優先降低整體半導體曝險，使基本面良好的企業亦受到波及。但中期而言，企業
獲利成長、技術競爭力、訂單能見度及自由現金流仍將決定股價表現。先進製程、先
進封裝與高階記憶體等產業仍具 AI需求支撐，惟須持續追蹤主要客戶資本支出與訂單
變化。
4. 美國對中 AI管制趨嚴，全球 AI投資需求可能出現區域性轉移：美國對中 AI出口管制
範圍擴大，短期可能增加第三國資料中心建置及半導體供應鏈出貨的不確定性，但不
必然代表全球 AI運算需求同步下降。若部分資料中心投資計畫因許可審查而延後，資

本支出可能逐步轉向美國及其他合規風險較低的市場，使不同地區及企業的受惠程度

### Page 4

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
出現差異。大型美系雲端業者若具備既有資料中心據點、充足資金及較完善的合規能
力，中期可能具有相對競爭優勢。後續應關注出口許可審查進度、大型雲端業者的區
域投資布局，以及台灣供應鏈訂單是否出現轉移或取消，以判斷政策影響主要為需求
重新分配，或已造成整體需求下降。
5. AI信用風險重要性提高，尚不宜判定為全面性信用危機：大型雲端業者通常擁有成熟
業務及穩定營業現金流，新興資料中心營運商則較依賴外部融資。隨著市場開始關注
晶片供應商提供客戶信用支持的情況，未來除追蹤高槓桿企業，亦須檢視大型科技企
業的擔保責任及潛在財務曝險。我們認為，若 AI相關企業信用利差持續擴大，且風險

進一步擴散至整體投資級公司債市場，將是需要下修風險資產配置的重要訊號。
6. 避險需求支撐美債表現，短期尚須等待止穩訊號：隨著全球科技股賣壓加劇，投資人
避險需求明顯升高，帶動美國 10年期公債殖利率下跌約 10個基點，重新回落至 5%
以下。後續美國通膨、就業數據及 12月 FOMC會議仍將影響全球金融市場方向。若
Fed 緊縮幅度超出市場預期，科技股可能同時面臨折現率偏高及獲利成長放緩的壓力
。我們認為，市場真正止穩須觀察輝達財務疑慮是否釐清、半導體企業獲利預估是否
回穩、信用利差是否停止擴大，以及市場廣度與外資流向是否改善。在上述訊號尚未
明朗前，短期股市恐仍維持高波動。
四、 市場展望與投資建議：
1. 短線審慎，中期審慎偏正向，關注企業獲利與資金面變化：本波全球科技股修正已由
初期的評價調整，進一步擴大至企業治理、政策風險及資金面的全面性重新定價。尤
其費半指數單日重挫超過 10%，顯示市場風險偏好快速下降，短期賣壓尚未完全解除
。然而，目前尚無充分證據顯示 AI運算需求已出現全面性反轉，輝達最新財報及主要

CSP持續投入 AI基礎建設，仍可為產業中長期成長提供一定支撐。因此，我們對美國
科技股及具備 AI供應鏈優勢的台灣、日本市場，短期維持審慎，中期則採取審慎偏正
向的觀點。不過，科技股未來表現將更依賴實際獲利、訂單能見度及自由現金流，而
非單純的 AI題材。後續應持續關注輝達 SEC調查、CSP資本支出、Fed政策及信用
風險是否擴散；若相關疑慮逐步釐清、企業獲利預估保持穩定，股市可望逐漸回歸基
本面主導。策略上，對中長期仍看好科技產業的投資人，可透過以分批布局及分散類
股配置方式參與，在科技類股部分，應優先考量具備技術競爭力、穩定現金流與較佳
財務體質的企業，避免過度集中於高度依賴外部融資的 AI業者。
2. 美債避險功能重新受到重視，中天期風險報酬相對均衡：Fed升息預期仍使債券市場
面臨利率波動，但隨著全球股市風險偏好下降，美國公債的避險需求已明顯升高。11
月 20日美股重挫，10年期美債殖利率大幅下降，顯示部分資金轉向相對安全的固定
收益資產。儘管 Fed政策尚未明朗，且通膨仍可能造成殖利率反覆波動，但目前美國

公債殖利率仍具有一定吸引力與防禦價值。從資產類別角度來看，我們對中天期美債
維持相對正向看法，主要考量其殖利率水準及利率敏感度的風險報酬較為均衡；超長

市場焦點
美國經濟
• 第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。美國9月個人消
費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，年增3.6%；核心PCE指數月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對
於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，
持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
期中選舉
• 美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，
將提高川普後續推動政策的難度。觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年
通常是股市表現最佳的年度，其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對

政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
中國
• 9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快，中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI
硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企業則是能
源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲
弱與價格競爭問題未解，投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。

美國第三季GDP成長超預期、通膨壓力續增
• 美國第三季經濟成長率(GDP)創一年來最大增幅：美國經濟分析局(BEA)公布，第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場
估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。企業AI投資火熱、消費者開支保持韌性，抵銷了油價上漲衝擊。
• 9月數據顯示通膨壓力續增，但符合預期：美國9月個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，寫3月以來最大月增幅；年增幅
3.6%。剔除食物與能源的9月核心PCE指數，月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 市場反應：由於新一輪通膨數據符合預期，市場未改變聯準會12月利率決議升息一碼的預測。然而，有跡象顯示實質個人
消費支出開始轉弱，儲蓄率也有所下降，市場開始押注通膨可能已經見頂。從聯邦利率期貨市場數據來看，市場對於明年升

息空間的預期已較上個月收斂(九月底預測升息2~3碼→目前預測1~2碼)，
• 觀點：美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，
可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。

聯準會貨幣決議與聲明稿差異
資料來源：Federal Reserve（10/28、9/16 聲明）；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。以上為聲明重點整理。
2 / 5
10月決議｜按兵不動
3.75% – 4.00%
10票贊成、2票反對
經濟措辭轉強 「穩健擴張」改為「強勁擴張」；國內支出具韌性， 生產力與資本投資表現強。
通膨仍未解除 聲明維持「通膨居高」的判斷，未再宣稱本次升息有 助加快回到2%目標。
政策訊號更具彈性 改稱需要更多資訊判斷適當政策立場，未承諾下一步 升息或降息。
關鍵變化
理事庫克(Lisa D. Cook) 和克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth M. Hammack)主張本次升息1碼，
形成2張鷹派反對票；9月決議則為一致通過。利率不變不等於轉向寬鬆，委員會內部對通膨風險仍

有不同判斷。
市場解讀：本次是「暫停觀察」；12月是否再升息，仍取決於通膨、需求與能源價格。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
資料來源：Federal Reserve 10/28會後記者會；Reuters、Investing.com，2026/10/28。
3 / 5
01｜市場利率反映緊縮壓力
華許指出，9月與10月兩次會議之
間，美國公債名目與實質殖利率明
顯走高；市場利率可先行反映成長
、通膨與風險變化，不必等政策利
率調整。
政策含意
長天期殖利率仍受通膨預期、期限
溢酬及公債供需影響；政策利率不
變，不代表債市壓力解除。
02｜AI投資與生產力
華許認為美國經濟尚未過熱，AI投
資推升需求，但生產力也有改善；
聯準會將持續評估新科技對產出、
就業與物價的中長期影響。
政策含意
AI支出可支撐企業成長，也伴隨資本
支出回收與融資風險；應檢視現金流
、投資報酬及生產力是否實際提升。

03｜政策制度與工具再檢視
聯準會規劃2027年安排1次、2028
年安排2次專注貨幣政策策略的會
議，不進行利率表決或發布政策聲
明；另將檢視資產負債表政策。
政策含意
制度調整不等於政策轉向寬鬆；仍須
觀察準備金、資產負債表操作及經濟
數據，評估金融條件如何變化。
解讀：華許除談利率外，也聚焦市場利率、AI生產力及政策制度調整；投資判斷仍應回到數據、企業獲利與金融條件。

聯準會利率決議摘要暨投資策略建議
資料來源：Federal Reserve；Reuters、InvestingLive；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。市場價格可能隨後續交易更新。
4 / 5
政策決議 利率維持 3.75% – 4.00% 經濟強、通膨高；政策下一步保留彈性
美股
短線受利率預期與科技股評價牽動
；AI投資支撐獲利，但仍需檢視現
金流與資本支出回報。
美債
若升息預期升溫，短天期殖利率較
敏感；長天期亦受通膨預期、發債
供給與期限溢酬影響。
美元／黃金
美元可能隨利差預期波動；黃金同
時受實質利率、美元及地緣政治避
險需求影響。
投資策略建議｜情境式配置
股票 保留核心持股，避免追價；優先關注獲利能見度、現金流與定價能力較佳企業。
債券 以分散到期日與信用風險為主；可分批配置高品質債券，避免單押長天期利率下行。

多元資產 保留流動性與再平衡空間；若通膨再升、殖利率上行，分批進場並控管整體波動。
後續觀察：美國通膨與就業數據、能源價格、企業財報，以及市場對12月政策的重新定價。

• 美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政：美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，為川普第二任期上任以來首場全國性選
舉，除國會議員改選外，亦包括州長及其他公職人員選舉。若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，將提
高川普後續推動政策的難度。不過，川普仍可透過發布行政命令等方式，在一定程度上繞過國會的立法制衡，持續推動其政

策議程。
• 本次選舉結果可能耗費一週才能明朗：回顧2006至2022年近五屆美國期中選舉，其中四屆的參眾兩院控制權於投票當晚或
隔日即大致明朗，僅2022年因關鍵席次競爭激烈、郵寄選票計票耗時較長，直至投票後第8天才確認兩院席次歸屬。考量本
次參議院選情膠著，加上川普執政以來美國政治極化持續加劇，不排除本次選舉結果需耗費一週才能明朗，期間市場也可能
面臨額外的不確定性。

期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度
• 期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度：觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳
的年度。根據1940至2025年統計，標普500指數第三年平均報酬率達16.5%，高於第一年的8.3%、第二年的4.2%及第四年
的6.9%；中位數報酬率更達18.9%。其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，
且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
• 政黨與國會組成影響有限，基本面仍是關鍵： 進一步觀察政黨差異，1940年以來，第三年由共和黨總統執政時，標普500指
數平均報酬率約18.2%，高於民主黨總統執政時的14.9%，但兩者均呈現正報酬，顯示總統所屬政黨並非決定股市表現的唯

一因素。國會組成亦可能影響政策推動與市場預期，例如共和黨總統搭配民主黨國會時，第三年平均報酬率約15.6%；若國
會呈現混合執政，平均報酬率則達23.1%。

能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升
• 能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升：9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快。但前三季度累計增速
從1到8月的15.7%小幅放緩至15.5%，利潤改善主要反映了能源和AI硬體價格的上漲，其中AI硬體價格仍是高技術製造業利潤
的重要支撐。前三季度，高技術製造業利潤年成長52.0%，與1到8月54.7%的增速大體相當。拉動整體利潤成長約10個百分點，
貢獻率約三分之二。值得注意的是，利潤成長仍主要集中於少數相關行業，其他企業部門整體依然處境艱難，內需疲弱疊加價
格競爭激烈令利潤受到擠壓。
• 投資建議：中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企

業則是能源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲弱與價格競爭問題未解，
投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.27
能源、AI價格飆升提振中國相關行業利潤增速

中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
• 中國科技公司享受負殖利率差：中國在經濟成長放緩與寬鬆貨幣
政策下，借貸成本持續下降，10年期公債殖利率由年初1.86%降
至1.63%，使企業融資條件更加寬鬆，相較之下，美國受經濟韌
性與高利率政策影響，10年期公債殖利率升至約5%，企業整體融
資成本明顯提高。此外2025年中國大型科技企業在境內債券市場
融資規模有限，僅為約74億美元，而且支付的殖利率，平均比同
風 險 層 級 的 債 券 高 出18個 基 點，但現在溢 價 已 經 變 為 折 價 ，
2026年迄今，中國科技企業已在境內發債約100億美元，新發科
技債殖利率平均折價6個基點，顯示機構投資者信心發生了顯著轉
變，大幅降低了行業推進AI建設的資金成本，會有上述現象是由
於中國經濟通縮，難以承受更高利率，因此官方須維持較寬鬆的

金融條件，科技企業剛剛開始通過債券市場融資，預料債券將受
到投資者關注。
• 展望後市：低成本債券融資對中國科技行業構成利好，與IPO不
同，債券市場為企業提供了一個可重複利用的融資渠道，在股市
劇烈波動之際，對投資人是另一種資產配置，可優先關注具資金
優勢與應用場景的大型科技龍頭，以及受惠於AI算力需求成長的
伺服器、網通設備和資料中心產業。

AI產業鏈融資警訊：資金缺口正在擴散
• 資本支出與現金流的嚴重失衡：AI生態系中的巨額投資正考驗著企業，一旦企業營運現金流不足以支持基礎設施建設，就必
須高度依賴外部融資(發債或增資)，使其更容易受到資本市場波動與利率變化的衝擊。AI算力提供商資金缺口最為顯著，其
資本支出已接近營運現金流3倍(284%)，需要投入大額資金採購高端GPU並對外出租算力，數據中心資本支出佔現金流的比
例已從2022年11月(ChatGPT問世)1倍(104%)快速爬升至1.3倍(128%)，超大規模雲服務商資本支出占現金流比例已從2022
年底40%翻倍飆升至73%。在彭博研究統計的8家超大型雲業者中，出現自由現金流為負(資金缺口)公司數量已從2022年的
1家增加至4家(達50%)。
• 產業鏈錯綜複雜，風險具連鎖效應：從上游晶片製造商、硬體開發商、建築商、電力/公用事業，到中游的數據中心與雲端

服務商，再到下游的軟體與數據管理公司，其銷售與支付合約彼此高度緊密相連。當算力提供商或數據中心出現資金吃緊時，
風險會迅速向上游傳遞，導致項目延期、融資成本攀升，甚至延後整個生態系的收入實現時程。目前除了雲端與數據中心外，
硬體開發商與建築商中出現資金缺口的企業比例也在上升。
資金缺口正蔓延至人工智能產業鏈一半的層級
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
算力提供商和數據中心的資本支出超過現金流

市場定價轉變：借貸成本上升與股票估值修正
• 資本市場已經開始針對資金缺口進行風險定價：在資金缺口最大的AI算力提供商、數據中心以及超大規模雲服務商中，其發
行的公司債相較於美國國債的殖利率差(利差)顯著拓寬，高於一般投資級債券或高收益債券的表現，顯示債券市場對其違約
與融資風險要求更高的風險補償。由於大量現金流被基礎設施建設掏空，且AI應用端的獲利回收速度未達市場預期，投資人
不再願意給予極高溢價，整體AI產業鏈的預期本益比出現明顯修正。
• 小結與投資建議：當前AI產業正在經歷基礎設施過渡期，資本密集的算力出租、數據中心建設正遭受強烈的資金擠壓，若未
來1~2年內下游應用端的商業化營收無法順利銜接，依賴高度槓桿與外部融資的企業將面臨嚴峻的流動性挑戰。對AI算力出
租商及部分高負債的數據中心開發商保持謹慎態度，一旦信貸市場收緊或借貸成本升高，利潤與營運將受重創。晶片製造商

的經營現金流持續成長，財務結構改善，選擇擁有強大訂價權與現金流護城河的上游半導體巨頭，安全邊際最高，電力與公
用事業資本支出佔現金流比例相對穩定且受惠於剛性需求。超大規模雲服務商中表現差異大，優先選擇資產負債表健全、資
產回報率高且擁有傳統核心業務能持續提供穩定現金流的科技巨頭，遠離需大量靠發債維持資本支出的個股。
超大規模雲服務商和數據中心的利差擴大
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣(DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

### Page 1

ab 17 November 2026
Global Research
UBS Global I/O Semiconductors
New US Export Controls on Cloud Access;
Assessing the Impact
Summary Thoughts
The US Dept. of Commerce Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS) posted a new rule
addressing remote access to advanced semiconductors. Following discussions with
industry experts (including a former Commerce Dept. official), we believe the rule  is
essentially attempting to accomplish the same thing as the AI Diffusion Rule issued by

the Biden Administration, but never put into effect. We would note that the rule was
"written by, and at the direction of the  Department of Treasury" - atypical for such a
rule, according to our experts. While we can't say for sure that this is in direct response
to the NVDA situation, the timing seems highly coincidental and we highlighted this risk
here and in our estimate cuts yesterday morning. We would make a few key points:
 Demand impact is hard to assess, but could be significant. Current export

control laws have not required a license  for shipments to key countries such as
Malaysia, Thailand, Indonesia, and Singapore (though we note  there is little room
for data centers in Singapore). As a result, we have seen a large increase in data
center construction in Malaysia in particular with recent press reports from the
NYT and Economist  (here and here) suggesting ~2GW of new data centers now
online in a single province in Malaysia with another ~10GW currently under

construction, and  total approved volume up to ~25GW with backing from both US
and China hyperscalers. On this basis, it seems fair to assume  a  run-rate for
shipments into Malaysia alone of a few GW/yr and the NYT article indicated that
about half of this is going to China-domiciled  companies. There is a carve-out in
the new rule for online retailers and social media companies but we would think
that US companies would err on the side of conservatism unless they can be

continuously assured of the end customer. Ergo, it is possible that this new rule
could strip 1-2GW out of current demand, with most of this likely being from
NVDA. At $30B+/GW, this is roughly consistent with our estimate of a 10%
demand impact, though NVDA should still be able to divert these shipments to
other customers to mitigate any near-term impact so we see this as more of a
longer-term issue especially if powered shell permitting gets more restrictive in the

US.
 Odds of MATCH Act passage have gone up, but timing remains a key Q. We
have written in the past about  MATCH  here and here. While the US may not want
to enforce additional export controls on the heels of this new cloud access rule,
our conversations with experts marked a clear shift in tone around the odds of the
MATCH Act passing vs. prior months - with the legislation potentially being
attached to the NDAA in this upcoming Congress. That being said, an expert firmly

believes that the disparity between what ASML, TEL, and other foreign equipment
suppliers currently sell into China vs. US SPE suppliers is not all that significant,
making the rule more of a point of tension with US allies such as the Netherlands
and Japan. Second, the goal of this legislation would be "level the playing field"
using the FDPR (Foreign Direct Product Rule) but there is a question of who would
determine which tools sold by the Dutch and Japanese suppliers are “equivalent”

to those currently restricted for US equipment suppliers. Third, a contemplated
ban on immersion DUV would make supporting trailing-edge fabs and lines in
China quite challenging, with potential spillover effects across the industry, even
outside China. Fourth, it raises the question of what incentives, if any, the Dutch
and Japanese governments would have to comply. Related to this, an expert
indicated that the use of FDPR by the USG could be more upsetting than tariffs

### Page 2

directly impacted by this rule, but in our coverage, we see KLAC being the only one
really indirectly negatively affected by the passage of this rule given its high attach
to litho process steps (and others affected to a lesser extent, though less litho
could in fact ripple to fewer dep/etch tools).
Read Across
We see NVDA as the most directly impacted by this rule, though the entire supply chain,
from memory through SPE, would ultimately be negatively affected by the closure of

such a large demand corridor. That said, we could argue that, over the longer term,
approving  the MATCH Act could benefit memory suppliers outside China by
constraining domestic Chinese capacity additions.

future course of unit sales, ASPs, and revenues.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

CHPT (6510.TWO)
25 November 2026 Citi Research
3
CHPT
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and the structural shift towards high-end
testing. Our target price of NT$4,000 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the company's average forward PE
since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical

integration model. With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play
(rather than a consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin

compression from lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated US AI GPU and ASIC programs;
3) intensifying competition in testing boards from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

participate when the analyst has a positive view of the company.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY)을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%)이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY)을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 109,000원 
현재주가 (10.29) 56,700원 
상승여력 92.2%

### Page 1

(034020) 
두산에너빌리티 
2026.10.30

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 80,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 56,700 원(10/29)

 [투자의견] 당사는 두산에너빌리티에 대해 목표주가를 80,000원(2027년 
BPS의 6.2배)으로 27% 하향하며, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 실적은 견조
한 회복을 보이고 있으나, 최근 GE Vernova(GEV) 등 글로벌 AI 및 에너지 
밸류체인 Peer 주가 조정을 반영해 멀티플을 하향 조정했다. 체코 시공 계
약 등 일부 노이즈에 의해 주가가 급락한 만큼 매수 기회라고 판단한다.  
 [3Q26 Review] 매출액 4.4조원(-5.8%qoq, +14.7%yoy), 영업이익 
2,612억원(-16.9%qoq, +90.6%yoy)을 기록해 당사 추정에 부합하는 무
난한 실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티 부문은 매출액 1.8조원, 영업이익 1,211
억원(영업이익률 6.6%, +2.3%p qoq)을 기록하며 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 
고수익 사업 믹스 개선으로 분기 수익성이 재차 개선됐다. 당기순이익은 
149억원(-93.4%qoq)에 그쳤으나, 원달러 환율 급락으로 발생한 환손실 
반영 때문으로 이익 체력과는 무관하다고 판단한다. 3Q26 미국 빅테크 데
이터센터향 가스터빈 첫 출하를 달성했으며, 수주잔고는 에너빌리티 부문 
기준 26.6조원(+70.9%yoy)으로 사상 최대치를 경신했다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 4.8조원, 영업이익 2,640억원으로 유사한 실적 
흐름을 전망한다. 4Q 가스터빈 인도와 체코 두코바니 원전 진행률 반영으로 
에너빌리티 부문 외형 성장이 기대된다. 3Q26 별도 기준 영업이익률이 
8.5%까지 개선된 만큼 에너빌리티 부문의 분기 실적 개선 추세는 유효하다
고 판단한다. 연간 수주 목표 13.3조원 달성을 위한 잔여 수주(3.3조원 +α)
가 예정되어 있으며, 대형원전을 제외하더라도 가이던스 달성은 무난할 것
이라 판단한다. 세부적으로는 SMR 1조원(계약 조건 합의 완료 단계로 연내 
체결 가능), 신재생 7,000억원(해상풍력 기자재·EPC 2건 협의 완료 단계), 
해외 복합 EPC 5,000억원(국내 1건, 해외 2건)이 기대된다. 
 [가스터빈] 3Q26 누적 가스터빈 13기·스팀터빈 11기·LTSA 6건을 달성하
며 연간 10기 이상 수주·수행 역량이 안정적으로 유지되고 있다. 2030년 
슬롯 예약 물량의 본계약 전환은 2027년, 2031년 슬롯은 2028년으로 예
상되며, 수주잔고 증가가 기대된다. 가스터빈 수요 증가를 감안하면 추가 캐
파 증설 결정이 이르면 연내 이루어질 전망이다. 
 [원전] 10월 NuScale 1개 모듈 제작 계약을 체결했으며, PPA 지연 여부와 
무관하게 완결된 계약으로 실적에 반영될 전망이다. X-energy는 연내 기자
재 및 부품의 후속 계약을 추진 중이다. TerraPower 주기기 3종 중 2종 계
약이 완료되어 제작에 착수했으며, Rolls-Royce SMR·i-SMR 등 파트너십 
확대도 지속되고 있다. 이외에 대미투자 관련 기자재 발주는 미국 부지 확정 
및 한국 의회 승인 절차가 남아있어 내년 발주가 기대된다. 기존 웨스팅하우
스 AP1000 Vogtle 계약 대비 업무 범위 확대(원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 
품목) 가능성이 있어 긍정적으로 평가한다.

### Page 2

두산에너빌리티

2_ Eugene Research Center 
설명회 Q&A 
Q1. 4Q26 SMR 수주 목표 약 1 조원의 실현 가능성 
- 고객사와 GTC 등 주요 계약 조건에 대한 합의를 대부분 완료하고 계약 체결 대기 중 
- 연내 계약 가능성이 있어 수주 목표는 의미 있는 수준

Q2. NuScale 모듈 1 기 제작 계약의 PPA 지연 영향 
- 완결된 제작 계약으로 PPA 체결이 지연되더라도 해당 모듈의 제작 및 진행에는 문제없음

Q3. AP1000 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 회계 처리 및 사업 영향 
- DOE·DOC 및 한국 정부 재원을 활용해 EPC 계약·착공 지연과 무관하게 기자재 제작 가능 
- 프로젝트 중단 시 웨스팅하우스의 다른 프로젝트로 기자재 전용 가능성 검토

Q4. 체코 원전 컨소시엄 변경 시 참여 및 사업 계획 
- 현지 지점 운영과 시공인력 상주로 시공 환경 자료 확보 
- 한수원과 초기 인허가·설계에 참여하고, 주기기 공급·설치 역량을 바탕으로 경쟁력 확보 기대

Q5. 체코 원전 EPC 사업의 수익성 확보 가능성 
- 한수원 가이드에 따라 수익성을 고려한 입찰 진행 예정

Q6. 웨스팅하우스 SPV 및 폴란드 AP1000 장주기 기자재 발주 진행 상황 
- DOE·DOC 프로젝트와 폴란드 AP1000 3 기 기자재 조기 발주 협의 중 
- 연내 수주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 수주 추진

Q7. 3Q26 영업이익률 개선 요인 
- 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 사업 믹스 개선이 수익성에 기여 
- 제작 진행률과 마일스톤에 따라 이익 인식

### Page 3

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _3 
Q8. 4Q26 복합화력 수주 목표 5,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 국내 1 건, 해외 2 건 협의 중이며 국내 프로젝트의 수주 가능성이 높은 상황 
- 대미 투자 물량은 기존 2031 년까지의 예약계약 납기와 비교해 검토 중

Q9. 3Q26 에너빌리티부문 당기순손실 원인 
- 급격한 환율 하락에 따른 약 800 억원의 환산손실이 주요 원인

Q10. 대미 투자 관련 기자재 발주 시점 
- 미국 부지 확정, 웨스팅하우스 사업제안 및 한국 정부 심사·국회 승인 절차 필요 
- 연말~내년 초 발주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 발주 추진

Q11. 대미 투자 프로젝트의 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 품목까지 공급 범위 확대를 위해 웨스팅하우스와 협상 중

Q12. 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 실적 영향 
- 기자재 출하 시 수금 조건이 충족되며 선발주 자금도 사전 확보 
- 보글·VC 서머 사례에서 기자재 대금을 회수해 프로젝트 지연에 따른 실적 영향은 제한적

Q13. 4Q26 신재생·풍력 수주 목표 약 7,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 해상풍력 기자재·EPC 계약 및 유지보수 계약 추진 중 
- 주요 계약 협의가 상당 부분 완료돼 일정 지연이 없다면 연내 목표 달성 전망

Q14. 중형 가스터빈 개발 및 해외 진출 계획 
- 대형 가스터빈은 한국·미국 중심으로 추진하며 중동 고객사와도 협의 중 
- 휴스턴 서비스 허브를 기반으로 미국 데이터센터 시장 대응 
- 중형 가스터빈은 시제품 제작 및 시험 진행 후 2028 년 하반기 출하 목표

### Page 4

두산에너빌리티

4_ Eugene Research Center 
Q15. NuScale 모듈 1 기 선발주 배경 
- 대규모 일괄 발주에 대비한 제작 경험 확보 및 납기 리스크 완화를 위해 사전 제작 추진

Q16. X-energy 연내 후속 계약 대상 품목 
- 증기발생기 전열관 및 주단 소재 등 장주기 품목의 사전 발주 추진

Q17. DOE 프로젝트의 장주기 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 웨스팅하우스 공급망 상황에 따라 추가 품목 협상 중 
- 과거 보글·VC 서머보다 넓은 공급 범위 기대

Q18. 웨스팅하우스 지분 투자와 기자재 계약 시점의 연계 가능성 
- 지분 투자는 내년, 기자재 계약은 연내 또는 내년 초 목표로 시점이 일치할지는 불확실

Q19. X-energy 장주기 기자재의 소재·부품 구분 
- 증기발생기 전열관은 부품, 주단 소재는 소재에 해당 
- 전열관은 공급 병목에 대비해 조기 발주하고 주단 소재는 설계 확정 후 제작 준비

Q20. 복합화력 주기기 수주의 기존·신규 고객 비중 
- 기존 고객과 추가 예약계약을 협의 중이며 신규 글로벌 고객과도 예약계약 협상 진행

Q21. 주요 SMR 사업 외 파트너십 확대 가능성 
- Rolls-Royce 사업 추진 및 GE Hitachi BWRX-300 주단 소재 예약계약 체결 
- i-SMR 은 2035 년 운전을 목표로 2028 년 이전 발주가 필요해 관련 협의 진행

Q22. X-energy 전열관·주단 소재 수주 물량 규모 
- 다우케미컬 초도 사업은 80MW 원자로 4 개 모듈로 구성되며, 해당 4 개 모듈분 부품·소재 공급

### Page 5

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _5 
Q23. 중동 지정학적 불안정에 따른 프로젝트·물류 영향 
- 기존 프로젝트의 운송비 증가분을 발주처·개발사와 협의 중 
- 신규 계약에는 비용 보상 및 공기 지연 관련 조항 반영

Q24. 가스터빈 생산능력 증설 규모 및 시기 
- 현재 생산능력 증설 중이며 2031 년 출하분까지 예약 접수 
- 시장 수요에 따라 추가 증설 검토

Q25. 2030 년 가스터빈 예약 물량의 실제 계약 전환 시점 
- 2030 년 출하 물량은 대부분 내년 실제 계약 여부 결정 전망 
- 2031 년 출하 물량은 2028 년경 계약 여부 결정 예상

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
 (2026.06.30 기준)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학

RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh 규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

2 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Setting the Stage for 2027
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Typically, a new administration sets the stage in their first year with stimulative policy
changes that play out in the second year after the new policies have been enacted into
law and begin to have their impact. This pattern shows up in historical data with the
average rate of gross domestic product (GDP) growth highest in the second year of

presidential terms compared to the other three years of the presidential term. At the
same time, equity market returns tend to be lowest on average in the second year of
presidential terms, which seems to be at odds with the fact that growth is strongest.
This apparent inconsistency can be attributed to the fact that the stock market is a
leading indicator that anticipates stronger growth and higher profits well before they
actually arrive. Once they do, as they have in 2026, the markets are looking ahead to the

inevitable slowing from the policy-induced peak in growth that typically arrives after the
midterm election year. The midterms also create uncertainty about the subsequent policy
environment which damps stocks ahead of the election only to be reversed on average
once the elections are over and the outlook is clarified. This phenomenon is seen in the
fact that on average the stock market has corrected about 20% ahead of midterms only
to rise about 30% from that bottom in the year after.

Q3 GDP data illustrate this pattern. Economic growth picked up through the first three
quarters of 2026 to a peak of 3.4%. Consumer spending growth averaged 3.8% over the
past six months, while the AI investment boom accelerated at a midteens growth rate.
Private sector purchases were growing at just shy of a 5% growth rate. GDP growth is
lower than that because imports of Asia produced technology-gear for the AI buildout
are subtracting a good bit from that underlying 5% domestic demand growth pace.

The new policies aimed at helping consumers through lower taxes and investment
incentives to spur private sector production have boosted growth as expected. Still, this
acceleration is passing, and the fiscal boost is likely to fade in 2027. Profits naturally
have followed this stronger GDP growth higher.
The upward trend in analysts' earnings revisions has started to roll over in our view, and,
while earnings are still growing, the rate of growth is likely to slow from here. In simple

terms, the second derivative of growth has turned negative. From way above trend
toward trend growth is still sufficient for the bull market to continue.
Another aspect of this transition to midcycle aside from the fading midterm
election boost from fiscal measures is the shift of monetary policy from maximum
accommodation to a more neutral policy. The Fed's rate cuts helped the midterm
acceleration in growth. Its shift to removing accommodation with rate hikes this year

adds to the odds of some momentum loss in 2027. That's already apparent as Federal
Reserve (Fed) Chair Warsh noted in his press conference after the September Federal
Open Market Committee meeting. Weaker balance sheet borrowers have seen their
spreads blow out as credit becomes more expensive. So far, the damage is confined to
the lowest-quality borrowers. Before, these spreads were unusually low reflecting easy
money conditions that are now normalizing. It's still early days in the new credit cycle.

Of course, how the market responds to this new Fed tightening cycle depends on how
aggressively the Fed raises rates. Analysts at Ned Davis Research note the S&P 500
has gained an average of 4.4% in the first year of all postwar tightening cycles. This
outcome varies considerably depending on the extent of the tightening. Aggressive
tightening cycles have seen an average decline of 3.6% a year later, whereas gradual
tightenings have seen a 10.5% average gain. In short, as one might expect, too much

tightening can cause a decline in stocks. Currently, the market seems to anticipate a
Investment Implications
Equities should continue to outperform
bonds in 2027 in our view, but
performance is likely to shift in favor of
higher-quality, less leveraged segments
of the market as financial conditions
tighten and earnings momentum slows,
while the accelerating momentum of
the early cycle transitions to midcycle
cruise control.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
December and March nudge up in rates to reverse the easings by the Powell Fed. This
would be consistent with an ongoing equity bull market in 2027. Obviously, inflation
will have a lot to say about whether a mild or aggressive tightening cycle is called for.
With wage pressures benign, productivity strong and the early-cycle acceleration set to
peak, we believe the odds of an aggressive tightening cycle that derails the bull market

are low.
Other policy effects have shaped the current economic cycle. The shift toward more
nationalistic trade policies with tariffs, for example, has drastically impacted the pattern
of trade flows. U.S. imports from China have dropped from about 20% 10 years ago to
less than 10%, according to research from Strategas. Nevertheless, contrary to concerns
of a trade-induced recession, overall trade has continued to grow in line with the global
economy. Rather than deglobalization, the world is undergoing a reglobalization into new

trade spheres that better align with national interests.
According to McKinsey Quarterly, "The 2025 U.S. National Security Strategy, for
example, reinforces economic and supply chain security, industrial capacity, technological
leadership, and capital market strength as national priorities. China's latest five-year plan,
meanwhile, includes technological self-reliance, economic stability and the modernization
of core industries among its key pillars. In this environment, value creation is increasingly

shaped by new trade corridors, growing defense spending, expansion of export controls
and industrial policies, and shifting flows of foreign investment."1 These new trade
policies are an impetus for growth that is structural, helping to make for a strong
investment cycle.
Obviously, AI is a big spur for investment that has been facilitated by the new policies
such as accelerated depreciation of capital expenditures (capex). McKinsey Research
designates 18 arenas with cutting-edge industries they believe are driving the global

growth story for the next couple of decades. They note companies headquartered in
the U.S. and China account for about 90% of these arenas' market value today. U.S.
companies account for 75% of the market capitalization within the 18 arenas and
generate about 50% of revenues. Given its leading role in the arenas driving growth
into the future, we believe the U.S. is well poised to outperform as the world moves
into this new technological future. The 18 arenas are part of the economy growing by

several multiples of the old economy segments and help explain why U.S. Equities have a
persistent valuation premium over the rest of the world.
Because of the unusually rapid growth of these new technologies associated with the
singularity, while earnings growth may decelerate from its peak, there are structural
reasons to believe earnings are on a new, higher-growth trajectory and unlikely to revert
fully back to historical norms. The new technologies have much higher margins than the

old economy sectors. The combination of faster growth and higher margins bodes well
for the equity market as these new arenas of future economic growth become a bigger
part of the U.S. and global economies.
The midterms have changed views about the relative performance of some segments
of the economy. For example, strong sentiment to slow the buildout of AI data centers
was dramatically evident in the election results. Beneficiaries of the buildout are being

revalued to reflect this slower growth. Disapproval of the Iran War was also registered
in the results. Energy prices have dropped on hopes for a quicker end to the war. This in
turn has lowered inflation expectations and interest rates. Overall, while the midterms
have remixed relative performance between sectors they have not changed the outlook
for continued solid growth and an ongoing bull market in equities.
1 McKinsey Quarterly, Q3 2026, page 52.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
China, the WTO and the Great Convergence No One Expected
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Twenty-five years ago this December, the West made one of the biggest economic bets
of the modern era: It brought China into the WTO, convinced that membership would
transform the communist giant into a more open and market-oriented economy.
The logic seemed impeccable at the time. The Soviet Union had long been vanquished.

American capitalism appeared triumphant. Free trade, deregulation, privatization and
unfettered cross-border flows of goods, services, capital and labor were widely viewed as
essential ingredients of economic prosperity. Globalization ruled.
Fold China into the rules-based system, the thinking went, and economic liberalization
would gradually pull Beijing toward Western norms. China would become a willing
participant in the U.S.-led global economic order. The world would change China, in

other words.
Not quite. At the dawn of the 25th anniversary of China’s WTO accession, the great irony is
not how China became more like the West. It is how the West became more like China.
In the U.S., and across Europe and Asia, governments are increasingly embracing the
elements of China’s economic playbook, which range from state subsidies to the promotion
of national champions to strategic industrial policies, among other initiatives antithetical to

the global economic norms that prevailed when China joined the WTO.
The deployment of trade tariffs, the subsidization of semiconductor production, the
promotion of domestic manufacturing, and the screening of foreign investment are no
longer hallmarks of China alone. They are increasingly features of economic policymaking
in the U.S., the one-time champion of globalization. The European Union, Japan, South
Korea and other nations that once carried the flag for free trade and open markets have

moved much in the same direction. Even the World Bank—a chief architect and guardian of
Western-led globalization—has come around to blessing the merits of industrial policy, a
strategy central to China’s economic ascent.
To be sure, Washington has not embraced China’s economic model wholesale. Neither have
Brussels, Tokyo or Seoul. The political, economic and institutional differences between
these systems and China’s remain vast. But the direction—set by China—is unmistakable.

It was not supposed to be this way. To gain admission to the WTO, China made significant
concessions over 15 years of negotiations. Beijing slashed tariffs on industrial goods and
agricultural products. Various sectors—manufacturing, distribution, telecommunications,
banking and autos—were gradually opened to foreign firms. Commercial laws and regulations
were amended or written, including rules governing intellectual property rights. Nontariff
barriers like quotas, import licenses, dual pricing and opaque industry standards were either

eliminated or phased out. State-owned enterprises were reformed and restructured.
These concessions—and more—were taken as signs that China was serious about
becoming a global stakeholder. It stoked optimism among senior U.S. officials that China
was inevitably bound to converge and embrace Western economic norms. And, for a while,
the bet seemed to pay off.
As China lowered trade and investment barriers at the beginning of the century, U.S. goods

exports soared. American firms poured capital into the mainland, attracted by an enormous
labor pool, lower production costs and what would soon become one of the largest
consumer markets in the world.
China’s massive labor pool-cum-ballooning middle class paid off handsomely for Corporate
America. U.S. affiliate income earned in China rose from just $1.6 billion (B) in 2001 to
nearly $9B by 2010, making China one of the most profitable markets in the world for U.S.

goods and services. American consumers, meanwhile, enjoyed the benefits of low-cost
China imports. And debt-laden America benefited as China recycled its excess savings into
U.S. Treasurys, helping to grease the financial wheels of America.
Investment Implications
We expect China will continue to rely
on exports and investment to drive
growth amid weak consumption and
domestic demand. Manufacturing-
heavy economies like Germany
remain particularly exposed,

reinforcing our neutral stance toward
Europe in portfolios.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But just as WTO accession helped boost China’s imports, it also supercharged China’s
export machine. Rather than shrink, as some optimists had predicted, America’s
merchandise trade deficit with China more than doubled between 2001 ($83.2B) and 2005
($202.8B) and continued rising thereafter. As the decade wore on, U.S. manufacturing jobs
and wages came under intense pressure owing to expanding China imports and the shift

in U.S. production to lower-cost China. Entire supply chains migrated across the Pacific,
decimating many manufacturing communities across the U.S.
China began producing and exporting at a scale the world had never experienced. In the
span of just a decade, China’s share of world manufacturing output soared from 8.7%
in 2000 to 18.1% in 2010, when the nation eclipsed the U.S. as the world’s largest
manufacturer of goods. Four years later, China was outproducing all of Europe—and has

never looked back (Exhibit 1A).
Today, China sits at the center of global manufacturing and trade, competing not simply in
textiles, toys and other low-value products but also in more technology-driven industries
like electric vehicles, drones, machinery and clean energy. China now accounts for roughly
28% of global manufacturing, according to the World Bank, well ahead of second place
America (17%). Meanwhile, the nation’s share of world exports—just 4% in 2001—now

stands at 15%, roughly on par with the combined market share of second-place America
and Germany (Exhibit 1B). The world is so awash with China goods that the nation is on
course to post another annual merchandise trade surplus in excess of $1 trillion.
Not surprisingly, China’s grip on world trade has significantly dimmed the brimming
optimism that greeted the nation’s WTO entry a quarter-century ago. Today, the consensus
is that China’s trade surplus has been aided and abetted by nonmarket practices and

policies that include forced technology transfers, currency manipulations, state subsidies
to industry, restrictions on foreign investment in strategic sectors, intellectual property
infringements and resource protectionism. Rather than dismantle its state-led, nonmarket
and mercantilist economy, China has tacked in the other direction, notably under the
leadership of Xi Jinping.
For investors, the implications have been profound. We now live in a world where

governments increasingly decide which industries are strategically indispensable, which
technologies must remain domestically controlled and which companies require public
financing. The “Commanding Heights”—or who controls the economy—have shifted
from the private sector to the public sector. In this world, investment returns are not only
determined by traditional metrics like earnings, the cost of capital and valuations but also
by geopolitics, energy costs, trade protectionism and national security. The world today is

far different from the world when China joined the WTO.
Suffice it to say that the 25th anniversary of China’s entry into the WTO won’t be met
with much celebration in the West. The consensus in 2001 was that the world would
change China. Twenty-five years later, it’s China that has changed the world. It’s the great
convergence no one saw coming.

6 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are we reviewing to gauge the capital investment needed for the proposed Artificial
Intelligence buildout and the return required to cover its costs?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Capex by the 15 largest data center developers are expected to top $1.4 trillion in
2027, which is somewhere between 3.5% and 4% of U.S. GDP. A large portion of the
capital investment is earmarked for compute. The total capacity of gigawatts (GW)

under construction is around 34GW of Informaton Technology capacity, with the U.S.
accounting for approximately 70% of this, according to BloombergNEF. Given the
massive compute investment, we continue to analyze closely the total debt financing
announced, the approximate cost of capital of the capital providers, the associated
revenues needed to match or exceed cost estimates, and the individual credit spreads
(as well as credit default swaps spreads) of the larger hyperscalers and chip makers to

better assess the overall return on investment of the AI buildout.
BloombergNEF estimates, that at the beginning of this year, the highest-rated data
center bonds offered yields below 5%, which is now closer to around 7% today. In
addition, AI-tied firms have raised over $460B in debt financing this year, which is
more than double that from 2025. Finally, according to BloombergNEF and Bloomberg
Intelligence, as of September 18, 2026, it is estimated that the 15 largest publicly listed

data center companies would need to produce $2.5 trillion in revenues in 2032 in order
to achieve a return on invested capital equal to the company-level weighted average cost
of capital. This is about 5 times more than the expected revenue in 2026.
Recently, the concern across the capital markets is that there is not enough financing
capital to go around in order to fund the expected buildout, which is one of the primary
reasons why credit spreads have widened and yields have risen. We view this as a natural

development of such large-scale infrastructure transformations. This is the reason why
we continue to watch the overall health of balance sheets of the builders, the level of
profitability and margins, and the order backlogs/cancellations in the next few years. We
still believe we are closer to the middle of the AI buildout than to the end. Therefore, we
view larger-scale weakness in the higher-quality segments of the AI chain as potential
buying opportunities for investors who are underexposed to these areas.

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and the deep 
valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

### Page 6

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

### Page 8

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

### Page 9

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

7. Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts 
European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.

### Page 10

UK equities are resilient and defensive, Sterling has continued to strengthen. n
UK Gilts may be clearer outperformers given their highest beta to oil prices. n
European macro markets have had a tough year, primarily reflecting a negative terms of
trade shock, rate tightening without an offsetting investment spending boom, and most
recently the reappearance of sovereign stresses, especially in France. Even with the
repricing, there are still challenges: the energy risks are likely to be most acute going

through the winter, and the underlying fiscal issues in France are unlikely to be
adequately addressed until the French election in the Spring of 2027. As a result, using
the Euro as a funder for other crosses has been a popular position, and the skew of risks
and low carry continue to make downside optionality attractive for the near term. While
Europe remains hostage to global shocks, the main paths to relief also run through those
global channels—energy relief would be most helpful, as would lower yields in the rest of

core markets. Together they would also lessen the immediate pressure on fiscal budgets,
even though the underlying primary deficit would still need to be addressed. Any
slowdown in AI-related spending may also show European assets in a better relative light
given a much smaller share of tech in the region’s equity indices.
In those scenarios, the UK may shine even brighter. Gilts have had the highest beta to oil
prices, so a reduction in those pressures may provide the most relief to UK rates and to

the fiscal picture there. UK equity indices also have less tech exposure than the US
(although they do embed significant energy exposure), and so may also prove more
defensive in an AI-related spending slowdown and any associated market pressures. And
while the currency may also benefit from a softer Dollar backdrop, it is already the most
overvalued currency within our G10 coverage. Much has gone right for the Pound in
recent months—a smooth transition to a new Labour government, a ‘do-no-harm’

budget delivery, the BoE shifting to hikes, and encouraging talk of a closer settlement
with the EU. But we think further gains are likely to be more challenging, with our
monetary policy forecasts most dovish to market pricing in the UK.
12 November 2026   10
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 11

8. The stress of financing the boom 
AI investment financing has driven credit and equity issuance to cycle records. n
This has been digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit that has n
led spreads wider. 
A further widening in US spreads looks likely and a higher chance of meaningful n
widening than meaningful tightening. 
Europe offers more risk premium; mortgages and structured credit give some refuge n
from corporate supply. 
Credit and equity issuance has been hitting fresh cycle records, and the financing needs 
of the AI investment boom mean that those needs are likely to build further in 2027. So 
far, that issuance has—for the most part—been digestible, albeit with discounted 
pricing, and the credit underperformance that we expected in 2026 because of these 
dynamics has only really become clear in the last few months. Balance sheets of the 
major borrowers are strong and while free cash flow positions for the hyperscalers have 
deteriorated sharply, the overall corporate sector is much more balanced. We expect 
continued bifurcation between AI-related issuers and “everything else”, with episodic 
indigestion of jumbo AI debt financing leading to wider spreads for AI-related sectors.  
We expect further widening in index-level credit spreads in 2027, though in our base 
case the scope is fairly modest. The risks around that view are skewed though. It is hard 
to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is more scope for 
tightening in Europe where the risk premium has increased more), and easier to see how 
spreads could move wider, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate more than we 
expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability of the AI 
investment boom. We expect modest US HY underperformance (decompression) in our

Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 12

baseline, but the risk is that problems in the left tail of weaker borrowers create more 
turbulent conditions for broader financing. Mortgages and structured credit markets 
may offer some refuge from the heavy corporate supply. From 1998 to 2000, there was 
sustained widening in credit spreads and substantial decoupling between credit and 
equity markets. Without the large capital inflows and goods price deflation from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998, we think that kind of divergence will be harder now.

9. EM—resilience and diversification 
2026 is one more year when EM assets have shown resilience/outperformance in the n
face of external shocks. 
But the flip side is less compelling valuations and lower carry, despite friendly macro. n
So the case for EM investing is exposure to high, diversified and resilient yields in EM n
fixed income ... 
... and a regional and sectorally diversified earnings mix in EM equities.  n
EM assets have again demonstrated impressive resilience in 2026 in the face of shocks 
from high energy prices and the spikes in core rates. EM rates have outperformed core 
rates, EM FX total returns are outpacing G9 and EM equities are on track for a narrower 
but still double-digit year after 30% gains in 2025. The upshot of this resilience and 
outperformance is that in many places valuations are less compelling than before. EM 
local rate spreads are tight relative to core rates, EM credit spreads are the tightest in 
years despite attractive all-in yields, and EM FX carry and valuations are firmly 
middle-of-the-road. 
Even though the baseline macro backdrop looks friendly for EM investing and 
carry-harvesting in 2027, less attractive valuations and carry levels mean that it is about 
gaining exposure to high diversified yields in both hard and local currency fixed income. 
In many cases, such as South Africa, Hungary, Colombia and India, those higher yields 
come with improving fiscal and structural reform stories. Likewise, in the case of EM 
equities, while the major markets are not expensive, much of the value in the headline 
index is an artefact of the surge in AI earnings that will be hard to repeat. Rather, the 
case for EM equity length is also about gaining exposure to a diversified earnings mix.

### Page 13

The EM equity index, especially the less concentrated, equal-weighted flavours, provide 
exposure not just to the tech and AI complex in North Asia, but also to cyclical and 
commodity-related growth in South Africa and LatAm, and to larger domestic demand 
stories such as India. And if we see a larger move lower in core rates or the Dollar than 
our baseline expectations, EM local assets provide upside convexity that is harder to 
replicate elsewhere.

10. Growth risks and tail hedges 
A benign baseline, but growth risks now skew to the downside. n
Diversification benefits from bonds, commodities and equity selection. n
Equity index volatility has scope to rise further. n
Downside in rates, USD/JPY, upside in energy and protection in equity options. n
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns across equities and bonds. But with a maturing market cycle and significant 
uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we continue to emphasize the value of 
diversification and hedges to protect against key risks. Some of those risks—from higher 
oil prices or sticky core inflation—are likely to push rates higher. But a growing number—
a sharper reversal in the AI boom, cybersecurity fears, AI-related labour-shedding or 
financial stresses—would push both growth and rate expectations lower. The further we 
move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for bonds, as the 
threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment tailwind 
recede. Diversification remains valuable in this environment. As we have said, the value 
of bonds as a diversifying asset is returning as the skew of risks shifts. And 
commodities—especially energy—continue to play a hedging role for portfolios, 
particularly when implied risk premia fall as they have recently.  
Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling

broadly. For near-term energy upside, hedges in European gas futures and TTF call

Document 375: Minutes of ECB’s October meeting confirm tightening bias

Source PDF: all report pdf/Minutes of ECB’s October meeting confirm tightening bias.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

The minutes of the ECB's October meeting conﬁrm a clear
tightening bias and suggest a rate hike at the December
meeting is almost a done deal
Carsten Brzeski
ECB President Christine Lagarde
The just-released minutes of the ECB’s October meeting conﬁrmed
the central bank's current tightening bias. Given that many ECB
members had already been in favour of a rate hike at the October
meeting, a December hike looks almost like a done deal. On a
slightly diﬀerent note, the minutes also shed new light on the ECB’s

internal arithmetic: a large number of members can still fall short of
a majority.
Here are the most important passages from the minutes:
Waiting until December to get a better picture of the longer-term
inﬂation forecast. "The December projections, which would extend
the forecast horizon to include 2029, would provide an updated
integrated assessment of how the energy shock, AI-related
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
